### 264. assistant

**Deep Review: PR #58 – "docs: multi-user 'assistant coach' platform architecture strategy"**

**PR Summary (as of the current state)**  
This is a **docs-only** PR that introduces `docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md` (a ~537-line research + strategy document) and makes targeted updates to `docs/COMMERCIALIZATION.md` (reorganizing the dashboard with explicit P0/P1/P2 priorities and adding C14 for owned-model licensing/training policy).

The proposal maps the evolution from the current **single-user, local, synchronous** tool to a **multi-tenant "assistant coach" platform** where:
- Users (academies/coaches/athletes) upload videos and get **persisted, browsable highlights + history**.
- Storage and compute are **decoupled and pluggable/BYO** (the core organizing principle).
- The product north star shifts from "clipper" to **shot detection + gait/biomechanics feedback under a supervising coach**.

Key technical proposals:
- **Control-plane / data-plane split** (orchestrator handles metadata/jobs; heavy bytes/GPU work can live elsewhere).
- Two new pluggable seams using the existing registry pattern:
  - `StorageBackend` (fsspec + smart_open; local/Drive/S3/GCS/Azure; **BYO-storage** via STS AssumeRole + presigned URLs so raw video never flows through our servers long-term).
  - `ComputeBackend` (local / hosted / **byo_worker** pull-based outbound worker + SkyPilot for on-demand in the user's cloud).
- Coach-first multi-tenant data model (org/academy → coach → athlete → user); `Highlight` becomes a first-class persisted entity.
- Tenancy: Postgres + Row-Level Security (hosted) or SQLite-per-tenant (self-hosted).
- Orchestration: Hatchet (durable, Postgres-backed jobs).
- Privacy model: shot/highlight detection treated as general analytics; gait/biometric template building treated as sensitive (Art. 9); emphasis on explicit consent, minors/parental consent, and BYO-storage as a liability shield.
- Differentiator: true **"your video, your storage, your compute"** (none of the main competitors offer this cleanly; SwingVision's edge case is noted).

Phased roadmap is sketched (P0 async + basic tenancy → ... → P4 assistant-coach features). The doc explicitly scopes itself as **strategy only** (no code), references 41 sources (fact vs. synthesis tagged), and ties into existing artifacts (`COMMERCIALIZATION.md`, `DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md` #16 for async, `MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md`, `PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md`, etc.).

The COMMERCIALIZATION.md update re-prioritizes the dashboard (new C14 on owned-model base licensing + training-data policy) and groups items by priority + status.

### Strengths

- **Strong continuity with the existing codebase.** The proposal correctly identifies that the *pipeline* (segmenters, providers, validators, sports, annotations) is already pluggable via registries and should be left largely untouched. Adding `StorageBackend` and `ComputeBackend` as peer registries is the most natural extension of the current design. This is disciplined and low-regret in spirit.

- **Privacy and liability thinking is unusually mature.** Calling out the distinction between "general analytics" (shots/highlights) and "biometric data" (gait templates) + the explicit consent ledger + minors handling + BYO-storage as the liability lever shows real diligence. This is often the part that kills sports-tech companies in regulated markets. The competitive note (no one else offers true BYO) is also well-grounded.

- **Good scoping and humility.** The doc repeatedly says "strategy only," "does not start implementation," and defers the first real infra slice (#16 async) pending your review. It acknowledges the coach/athlete model as a "market-fit hypothesis that will iterate." This matches the careful, checkpoint-driven approach you've used on the scaffolding work.

- **Practical BYO design choices.** The pull-based worker pattern (GitHub self-hosted runner style) + presigned URLs + AssumeRole is the right security posture. SkyPilot as the "provision in user's cloud" path is a reasonable on-ramp. Using fsspec + smart_open for the storage matrix is pragmatic.

- **Ties the threads together.** It properly connects to the commercialization constraints (C4 paid-LLM no-train, C12 SaaS foundations), the value layer work (#51), input-quality reports (#50), and the data flywheel.

### Areas of Concern / Open Questions

**1. Operational reality of the BYO model (the biggest practical risk)**  
The "your storage, your compute" differentiator is powerful on paper, especially for privacy-sensitive Indian academies. However, "run this small agent on your VM/GPU and point it at your bucket" is a non-trivial operational burden for the target buyer (academy coaches, not SREs). 

The pull-based design is credential-safe, but support, updates, monitoring, and "it stopped working after their IT changed the firewall" will be real. Has the model been pressure-tested against a non-technical academy admin? A "zero-infra BYO" path (narrow audited proxy with strict per-tenant isolation) might be needed as a middle tier.

**2. Migration & coexistence for existing users is underspecified**  
Today's data model (MD5-keyed `videos` in a single SQLite, output files in `output/`, no ownership) has to evolve to owned highlights, per-athlete history, coach→athlete links, etc.

The doc describes the target but not the transition:
- How do we attach identity to existing local data?
- Do we treat the current single-user as the first "coach" in a one-time import?
- What happens to already-compiled highlight videos that only exist as files?

This needs at least a short "Migration & coexistence" subsection (even if high-level).

**3. Orchestration choice (Hatchet) feels early and lightly justified**  
Hatchet is positioned as "the multi-user superset of the deferred hardening plan." It has nice properties (Postgres-backed durable execution, resumable long GPU jobs), but it is still relatively young compared to Temporal, a simple robust queue + worker, or even a lightweight Celery/RQ + Postgres setup.

Before treating this as the direction, a short "why this over the most obvious lighter alternatives" (with failure-mode tradeoffs) would strengthen the doc. The first async slice (#16) should probably validate the execution model rather than assume Hatchet.

**4. Scope creep vs. the "assistant coach" north star**  
The product framing (highlights as entry point → shot detection → gait/biomechanics under coach) is exciting and correctly identifies the buyer. However, the jump to full biometric feedback happens quickly.

Gait analysis that builds identifying templates is high-bar (consent, accuracy liability, model drift, regulatory). The doc should be explicit that the first version of "assistant coach" can (and probably should) be narrow — e.g., shot classification + basic feedback only — with full per-athlete gait templates as a later, consent-gated phase. Otherwise there's risk of over-promising in the strategy doc itself.

**5. Owned-model thread (C4 + new C14 + §5A) is getting very constraining**  
The updated commercialization table + platform doc are now tightly coupled around "fine-tuned open VLM (Qwen2.5-VL etc.) as primary, paid Gemini as fallback."

C14's constraints (Apache-2.0 bases only, never train on paid-LLM outputs, explicit per-clip consent ledger, exclude minors, etc.) are correct but extremely high-bar. The doc should clarify the **minimum viable first owned model** (e.g., "specialized shot detector / classifier only, not full generative video-QA") so the team doesn't accidentally sign up for training a general video-QA model before the consented flywheel is mature.

**6. Data model & serving layer details**  
The "generic core + coach overlay" is the right approach. However, the current `candidate_segments` + `play_segments` + events model will need non-trivial evolution once highlights become first-class and we have per-user history + coach correction loops feeding the golden set. The annotation registry + `candidate_segments` table are correctly called out as the seam for coach-correction, which is good.

### Specific Suggestions

- Add a short **"Operational model & support implications"** subsection under the BYO sections.
- Add a **"Migration & coexistence"** paragraph (4–6 bullets is enough).
- Make the Hatchet (or equivalent durable execution) choice more explicit and debatable.
- Add a clear "narrow first version of assistant coach" scoping statement under the product framing.
- In the commercialization dashboard, the new P0/P1/P2 grouping + C14 addition is an improvement in clarity. Consider whether C12 (SaaS foundations) should be bumped higher now that the platform strategy exists, or whether it stays P1 because the async slice is still gated.
- The 41-source research pass is impressive. A one-line "Key sources we are *not* relying on and why" (if any strong competing architectures were evaluated) would be valuable.

### Overall Recommendation

**Approve and merge** (docs-only, no code risk).

This is one of the better strategy documents in the repo. It correctly diagnoses that the hard part of multi-user is **not** the detection pipeline (already pluggable) but the **control-plane decoupling, credential isolation, privacy model, and operational model for the target buyer**.

The work is consistent with the scaffolding phase we just completed and with the commercialization constraints.

**Suggested PR comments** (you can copy/adapt):
- The BYO pull-worker + presigned model is the right security posture, but we need to be realistic about operational burden for academy admins. Consider adding a narrow "zero-infra BYO" proxy tier as a middle path.
- Please add a short Migration & coexistence subsection.
- The choice of Hatchet (vs. lighter durable execution or a simple robust queue) should be justified or turned into an explicit decision for the #16 slice.
- Strong on the privacy distinction and BYO-as-liability-lever. The consent ledger + minors handling will be load-bearing — good that it's called out early.
- The new C14 constraints + owned-model path are correct but very high-bar. Clarify the minimum viable first owned model (e.g. specialized shot classifier vs. full generative video-QA).

Would you like me to draft the exact GitHub review comments in a ready-to-post format, or dive deeper into any specific section (e.g., the StorageBackend interface vs. current `video_path` usage, the data model evolution from current SQLite, or the interplay with the deferred hardening plan #16)? 

I'm also happy to cross-check any part against the current codebase (e.g., how `StorageRef` could be introduced alongside the existing string path with minimal breakage).

diff --git a/docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md b/docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..ce1cf79
--- /dev/null
+++ b/docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md
@@ -0,0 +1,537 @@
+# Platform Architecture — Multi-User "Assistant Coach"
+
+**Status:** 📐 Strategy / research — for review. No code yet.
+**Date:** 2026-06-07 · **Owner:** avidullu
+
+**Purpose.** Map the path from today's **single-user, local** tool to a **multi-tenant
+"assistant coach" platform**: users upload videos, get highlights **stored and
+browsable by history**, and choose **where their data lives** (Google Drive / cloud
+bucket / local) and **where the compute runs** (our GPU / their VM). The organizing
+principle is to **decouple the layers** — identity, ingestion, storage, orchestration,
+execution/compute, the detection+highlight pipeline, and data-serving — behind small
+**pluggable interfaces**, reusing the registry pattern the codebase already uses for
+segmenters/providers/validators/sports/annotations.
+
+**Product framing (the north star).** This is an **assistant coach**, not just a
+clipper. Highlights are the *entry point*; the *target* is **shot detection + gait /
+biomechanics feedback under a supervising coach**. The buyer is the **academy/coach**
+(see the beachhead in [`PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md`](PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md)); **power users
+self-serve**. Two consequences ripple through this whole design: (1) the data model is
+**coach → athlete**, not just "a user with videos"; (2) gait/biomechanics is **biometric
+data**, which raises the privacy bar (see §7).
+
+> **Competitive note (validated by research):** none of Veo / Hudl / SwingVision /
+> Spiideo offer true BYO-storage or BYO-compute — they're vendor-cloud SaaS
+> (SwingVision uniquely runs *edge* compute on the user's iPhone). So **"your video,
+> your storage, your compute"** is a genuine differentiator for privacy-sensitive
+> academies — and SwingVision proves user-side compute is commercially viable. All four
+> share the same **teams → athletes → clips** data model, which we adopt (§4).
+
+> Scope: this is a **direction-setting doc**. It does not start implementation; the
+> first infra slice (an async job model) is the on-hold #16 in
+> [`DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md`](DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md). Commercial constraints come
+> from [`COMMERCIALIZATION.md`](COMMERCIALIZATION.md) (C4 paid-LLM no-train; C12 SaaS
+> foundations) and `archives/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md`.
+
+## 1. Current state & gaps (what we're evolving from)
+
+| Concern | Today | Gap for multi-user |
+|---|---|---|
+| Identity / tenancy | none | no users, orgs, auth, or isolation |
+| Storage | local `filepath` string; output to `output_dir` | no abstraction; can't BYO Drive/bucket; raw video must be on the box |
+| Data model | `videos` keyed by file **MD5**, `play_segments`, `events`, `candidate_segments` (SQLite) | no owner/athlete link; **highlights aren't a first-class persisted entity** (compile writes a file and returns a path); no per-user history |
+| Execution | pipeline runs **in-process, synchronously** in `/api/process` | blocks; can't dispatch to a user's VM/GPU; no job status |
+| Pipeline | **already pluggable** (segmenter/provider/validator/sport/annotation registries) | reuse as-is — this layer is the model for the rest |
+| API | `/api/process`, `/api/query`, `/api/compile`, `/api/config`; no auth | needs auth, per-tenant scoping, async job + history endpoints |
+
+Grounding: `backend/infrastructure/database.py` (schema + `PRAGMA user_version`
+migration ladder, v1), `backend/main.py` (endpoints, sync flow), the registry pattern
+in `backend/pipeline/segmenters/base.py::SegmenterRegistry` and
+`backend/providers/base.py`.
+
+## 2. Target layered architecture
+
+Decouple into layers with a clean **control-plane vs data-plane** split — the
+orchestrator coordinates *metadata and jobs*; the heavy *bytes and GPU work* can live
+elsewhere (even in the user's own cloud).
+
+```
+                ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
+   CONTROL      │  Presentation / API  (auth, per-tenant)        │
+   PLANE        │  Identity & Tenancy  (org→coach→athlete→user)  │
+                │  Orchestration / Jobs (submit→status→result)   │
+                └───────────────┬───────────────┬────────────────┘
+                                │ dispatch       │ metadata
+                ┌───────────────▼───────┐  ┌─────▼───────────────┐
+   DATA         │  Execution / Compute  │  │  Data / Serving     │
+   PLANE        │  (local | BYO-VM |    │  │  (per-tenant DB:    │
+                │   hosted GPU)         │  │   videos, segments, │
+                │   runs the Pipeline:  │  │   highlights, jobs, │
+                │   detect → highlight  │  │   history)          │
+                └───────────┬───────────┘  └─────────────────────┘
+                            │ read input / write artifacts
+                ┌───────────▼───────────────────────────────────┐
+                │  Storage  (local upload | Google Drive | S3 |  │
+                │  GCS | Azure)  — raw video + highlight outputs │
+                └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
+```
+
+The two **new pluggable seams** are **Storage** and **Compute/Execution**; everything
+else either exists (pipeline) or is net-new infra (identity, orchestration, serving). A
+**model/training layer** — the owned generative video-QA model (§5A) — sits atop these,
+fed by Storage (training corpus), Data (labels), and Compute (training + inference), and
+is delivered through the existing **AI-provider registry with a paid fallback**.
+
+## 3. Pluggable abstractions (extend the existing registry pattern)
+
+The codebase already proves the model: an `ABC` + a `Registry` singleton with
+`register/get/list_available` (e.g. `SegmenterRegistry`). Add two more registries with
+the same shape so they're plug-and-play and testable in isolation.
+
+### 3.1 `StorageBackend` registry
+Replace the bare `video_path: str` that flows through the pipeline with a
+**`StorageRef`** (a backend id + a key/URI), resolved by a backend.
+
+```python
+class StorageBackend(ABC):
+    name: str
+    def open_read(self, ref: StorageRef) -> BinaryIO: ...      # stream large video
+    def download_to_local(self, ref: StorageRef, dst: str): ...  # for ffmpeg/cv2/WSL
+    def upload(self, local_path: str, ref: StorageRef): ...     # persist highlight/artifacts
+    def signed_url(self, ref: StorageRef, ttl: int) -> str: ... # hand to LLM / browser
+    def stat(self, ref: StorageRef) -> StorageStat: ...
+    def list(self, prefix: StorageRef) -> list[StorageRef]: ...
+```
+
+- **Recommended implementation:** build the backend over **`fsspec`** (one matrix —
+  local / S3 (`s3fs`) / GCS (`gcsfs`) / Azure (`adlfs`) + Google Drive via `gdrivefs`,
+  Dropbox via `dropboxdrivefs`), reaching for **`smart_open`** as the robust large-MP4
+  byte-streaming primitive. Storage becomes one more registry. (Treat Drive/Dropbox as
+  add-ons — less battle-tested than `s3fs`/`gcsfs`; cover with integration tests.)
+- **Backends:** `local` (upload dir), `gdrive`, `s3`, `gcs`, `azure`. **BYO-storage**:
+  the tenant registers their own bucket/Drive; raw video **never leaves their account**.
+- **Auth — never persist users' long-lived keys.** For buckets, have the user create an
+  IAM role you **`AssumeRole`** into, scoped to one bucket/prefix, and mint **presigned
+  URLs** so raw video moves **browser ↔ the user's bucket directly, never through our
+  servers**. For Drive, OAuth with the narrow **`drive.file`** scope; for academies, a
+  **service account with domain-wide delegation**. Store any per-tenant creds encrypted.
+- **Why it matters for the pipeline:** ffmpeg/OpenCV/WSL need a **local file**, while the
+  LLM provider can take a **signed URL**. The backend exposes both `download_to_local`
+  (for compute) and `signed_url` (for the provider/browser), so the pipeline stops caring
+  *where* the bytes live.
+- **Migration path:** introduce `StorageRef` alongside the current local path; a `local`
+  backend wraps today's behavior so nothing breaks; adapters land incrementally.
+
+### 3.2 `ComputeBackend` / `ExecutionBackend` registry
+Where the pipeline actually runs for a given job.
+
+- **`local`** — in-process / local subprocess (today's behavior; dev + self-host).
+- **`hosted`** — our GPU VM pool (the SaaS default).
+- **`byo_worker`** — the tenant runs a **pull-based worker** (a small agent) on their own
+  VM/GPU. **Critical security property:** the worker *pulls* jobs scoped to its tenant
+  from the control plane, pulls input from the tenant's **own** storage, runs the
+  pipeline, and writes artifacts + results back — so **the orchestrator never holds the
+  user's cloud credentials** and needs **no inbound access** to their network. This
+  mirrors the **self-hosted CI runner** pattern (GitHub Actions runners use a 50s
+  outbound-only HTTPS long-poll). This pull-based runner is the **cheapest, most
+  credential-safe v1** for BYO-compute.
+- For users who want the platform to **provision GPUs in their *own* cloud account on
+  demand** (an academy with an AWS account but no standing GPU box), add **SkyPilot**
+  (BYOC: everything launches in the user's accounts/VPCs; creds stay with them).
+  **Modal** is the managed fallback for users wanting zero infra (accepting that raw
+  video then flows through Modal).
+
+The pipeline code is unchanged; only *where it's invoked* and *how it gets bytes* vary.
+
+### 3.3 Reuse, don't reinvent
+The existing `segmenter` / `provider` / `validator` / `sport` / `annotation` registries
+are the proven precedent and stay as the **pipeline** layer. The `annotation` registry +
+`candidate_segments` table are also the natural seam for the **coach-correction / golden
+-set** loop in the assistant-coach phase (§6, P4).
+
+## 4. Multi-tenant data model & serving layer
+
+### 4.1 Entities — generic core, with the coach model as an optional overlay
+The foundational schema is deliberately **domain-neutral** so we don't overfit to the
+coach mindset — the coach/athlete framing is a **market-fit hypothesis that will iterate**
+(see C13 / PMF), not a load-bearing assumption. **Generic core:**
+```
+User ──< VideoCollection ──< Video ──< Highlight ──< HighlightMetadata
+

No code yet.\n+**Date:** 2026-06-07 · **Owner:** avidullu\n+\n+**Purpose.** Map the path from today's **single-user, local** tool to a **multi-tenant\n+\"assistant coach\" platform**: users upload videos, get highlights **stored and\n+browsable by history**, and choose **where their data lives** (Google Drive / cloud\n+bucket / local) and **where the compute runs** (our GPU / their VM). The organizing\n+principle is to **decouple the layers** — identity, ingestion, storage, orchestration,\n+execution/compute, the detection+highlight pipeline, and data-serving — behind small\n+**pluggable interfaces**, reusing the registry pattern the codebase already uses for\n+segmenters/providers/validators/sports/annotations.\n+\n+**Product framing (the north star).** This is an **assistant coach**, not just a\n+clipper. Highlights are the *entry point*; the *target* is **shot detection + gait /\n+biomechanics feedback under a supervising coach**. The buyer is the **academy/coach**\n+(see the beachhead in [`PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md`](PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md)); **power users\n+self-serve**. Two consequences ripple through this whole design: (1) the data model is\n+**coach → athlete**, not just \"a user with videos\"; (2) gait/biomechanics is **biometric\n+data**, which raises the privacy bar (see §7).\n+\n+\u003e **Competitive note (validated by research):** none of Veo / Hudl / SwingVision /\n+\u003e Spiideo offer true BYO-storage or BYO-compute — they're vendor-cloud SaaS\n+\u003e (SwingVision uniquely runs *edge* compute on the user's iPhone). So **\"your video,\n+\u003e your storage, your compute\"** is a genuine differentiator for privacy-sensitive\n+\u003e academies — and SwingVision proves user-side compute is commercially viable. All four\n+\u003e share the same **teams → athletes → clips** data model, which we adopt (§4).\n+\n+\u003e Scope: this is a **direction-setting doc**. It does not start implementation; the\n+\u003e first infra slice (an async job model) is the on-hold #16 in\n+\u003e [`DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md`](DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md). Commercial constraints come\n+\u003e from [`COMMERCIALIZATION.md`](COMMERCIALIZATION.md) (C4 paid-LLM no-train; C12 SaaS\n+\u003e foundations) and `archives/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md`.\n+\n+## 1. Current state \u0026 gaps (what we're evolving from)\n+\n+| Concern | Today | Gap for multi-user |\n+|---|---|---|\n+| Identity / tenancy | none | no users, orgs, auth, or isolation |\n+| Storage | local `filepath` string; output to `output_dir` | no abstraction; can't BYO Drive/bucket; raw video must be on the box |\n+| Data model | `videos` keyed by file **MD5**, `play_segments`, `events`, `candidate_segments` (SQLite) | no owner/athlete link; **highlights aren't a first-class persisted entity** (compile writes a file and returns a path); no per-user history |\n+| Execution | pipeline runs **in-process, synchronously** in `/api/process` | blocks; can't dispatch to a user's VM/GPU; no job status |\n+| Pipeline | **already pluggable** (segmenter/provider/validator/sport/annotation registries) | reuse as-is — this layer is the model for the rest |\n+| API | `/api/process`, `/api/query`, `/api/compile`, `/api/config`; no auth | needs auth, per-tenant scoping, async job + history endpoints |\n+\n+Grounding: `backend/infrastructure/database.py` (schema + `PRAGMA user_version`\n+migration ladder, v1), `backend/main.py` (endpoints, sync flow), the registry pattern\n+in `backend/pipeline/segmenters/base.py::SegmenterRegistry` and\n+`backend/providers/base.py`.\n+\n+## 2. Target layered architecture\n+\n+Decouple into layers with a clean **control-plane vs data-plane** split — the\n+orchestrator coordinates *metadata and jobs*; the heavy *bytes and GPU work* can live\n+elsewhere (even in the user's own cloud).\n+\n+```\n+                ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n+   CONTROL      │  Presentation / API  (auth, per-tenant)        │\n+   PLANE        │  Identity \u0026 Tenancy  (org→coach→athlete→user)  │\n+                │  Orchestration / Jobs (submit→status→result)   │\n+                └───────────────┬───────────────┬────────────────┘\n+                                │ dispatch       │ metadata\n+                ┌───────────────▼───────┐  ┌─────▼───────────────┐\n+   DATA         │  Execution / Compute  │  │  Data / Serving     │\n+   PLANE        │  (local | BYO-VM |    │  │  (per-tenant DB:    │\n+                │   hosted GPU)         │  │   videos, segments, │\n+                │   runs the Pipeline:  │  │   highlights, jobs, │\n+                │   detect → highlight  │  │   history)          │\n+                └───────────┬───────────┘  └─────────────────────┘\n+                            │ read input / write artifacts\n+                ┌───────────▼───────────────────────────────────┐\n+                │  Storage  (local upload | Google Drive | S3 |  │\n+                │  GCS | Azure)  — raw video + highlight outputs │\n+                └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n+```\n+\n+The two **new pluggable seams** are **Storage** and **Compute/Execution**; everything\n+else either exists (pipeline) or is net-new infra (identity, orchestration, serving). A\n+**model/training layer** — the owned generative video-QA model (§5A) — sits atop these,\n+fed by Storage (training corpus), Data (labels), and Compute (training + inference), and\n+is delivered through the existing **AI-provider registry with a paid fallback**.\n+\n+## 3. Pluggable abstractions (extend the existing registry pattern)\n+\n+The codebase already proves the model: an `ABC` + a `Registry` singleton with\n+`register/get/list_available` (e.g. `SegmenterRegistry`). Add two more registries with\n+the same shape so they're plug-and-play and testable in isolation.\n+\n+### 3.1 `StorageBackend` registry\n+Replace the bare `video_path: str` that flows through the pipeline with a\n+**`StorageRef`** (a backend id + a key/URI), resolved by a backend.\n+\n+```python\n+class StorageBackend(ABC):\n+    name: str\n+    def open_read(self, ref: StorageRef) -\u003e BinaryIO: ...      # stream large video\n+    def download_to_local(self, ref: StorageRef, dst: str): ...  # for ffmpeg/cv2/WSL\n+    def upload(self, local_path: str, ref: StorageRef): ...     # persist highlight/artifacts\n+    def signed_url(self, ref: StorageRef, ttl: int) -\u003e str: ... # hand to LLM / browser\n+    def stat(self, ref: StorageRef) -\u003e StorageStat: ...\n+    def list(self, prefix: StorageRef) -\u003e list[StorageRef]: ...\n+```\n+\n+- **Recommended implementation:** build the backend over **`fsspec`** (one matrix —\n+  local / S3 (`s3fs`) / GCS (`gcsfs`) / Azure (`adlfs`) + Google Drive via `gdrivefs`,\n+  Dropbox via `dropboxdrivefs`), reaching for **`smart_open`** as the robust large-MP4\n+  byte-streaming primitive. Storage becomes one more registry. (Treat Drive/Dropbox as\n+  add-ons — less battle-tested than `s3fs`/`gcsfs`; cover with integration tests.)\n+- **Backends:** `local` (upload dir), `gdrive`, `s3`, `gcs`, `azure`. **BYO-storage**:\n+  the tenant registers their own bucket/Drive; raw video **never leaves their account**.\n+- **Auth — never persist users' long-lived keys.** For buckets, have the user create an\n+  IAM role you **`AssumeRole`** into, scoped to one bucket/prefix, and mint **presigned\n+  URLs** so raw video moves **browser ↔ the user's bucket directly, never through our\n+  servers**. For Drive, OAuth with the narrow **`drive.file`** scope; for academies, a\n+  **service account with domain-wide delegation**. Store any per-tenant creds encrypted.\n+- **Why it matters for the pipeline:** ffmpeg/OpenCV/WSL need a **local file**, while the\n+  LLM provider can take a **signed URL**. The backend exposes both `download_to_local`\n+  (for compute) and `signed_url` (for the provider/browser), so the pipeline stops caring\n+  *where* the bytes live.\n+- **Migration path:** introduce `StorageRef` alongside the current local path; a `local`\n+  backend wraps today's behavior so nothing breaks; adapters land incrementally.\n+\n+### 3.2 `ComputeBackend` / `ExecutionBackend` registry\n+Where the pipeline actually runs for a given job.\n+\n+- **`local`** — in-process / local subprocess (today's behavior; dev + self-host).\n+- **`hosted`** — our GPU VM pool (the SaaS default).\n+- **`byo_worker`** — the tenant runs a **pull-based worker** (a small agent) on their own\n+  VM/GPU. **Critical security property:** the worker *pulls* jobs scoped to its tenant\n+  from the control plane, pulls input from the tenant's **own** storage, runs the\n+  pipeline, and writes artifacts + results back — so **the orchestrator never holds the\n+  user's cloud credentials** and needs **no inbound access** to their network. This\n+  mirrors the **self-hosted CI runner** pattern (GitHub Actions runners use a 50s\n+  outbound-only HTTPS long-poll). ### 280. assistant

**Comments posted on PR #58.**

I used the GitHub tools to:

- Start a review.
- Add **three targeted inline review comments** directly on `docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md` (on the right/diff side of the new file) at the most relevant sections.
- Post a **top-level summary comment** (as a regular PR comment) that ties everything together, references the inline ones, and explicitly asks you to take another look after addressing.

This way the feedback is recorded in GitHub (attached to specific lines + the PR thread) and won't get dropped.

### What I posted (key excerpts for your convenience)

**Inline comment 1 — Product framing / v1 vs longer vision** (targeting the early "assistant coach, not just a clipper" + north-star paragraph):

> **Please make the v1 vs longer-term vision explicit (product framing + roadmap).**
>
> - **v1 scope (P1-P2)**: Shot detection, rallies, highlight video compilation, basic per-user history, coach correction feeding the golden set, and the entry-point "assistant coach lite" features.
> - **Longer vision (P4+)**: Full gait/biomechanics analysis, per-athlete identifying templates, rich semantic coaching feedback under supervising coach.
>
> The current text says "Highlights are the *entry point*; the *target* is **shot detection + gait / biomechanics**..." which blurs the line. The north star is inspiring, but for a phased strategy doc we need to be unambiguous that basic rally/highlight work is the concrete v1 deliverable, and the biometric/gait stuff is strictly future.
>
> This also helps set expectations when we unblock the async slice (#16) and start implementation.

**Inline comment 2 — Challenging Hatchet** (targeting the orchestration / Hatchet paragraph in §5):

> **Please challenge the Hatchet recommendation (section 5).**
>
> The requirements (idempotency + resumability for long GPU jobs) are valid.
>
> However, jumping straight to Hatchet feels early for a strategy doc:
> - Arq is already called out as the "lighter async-native FastAPI stopgap" — why not validate starting there for the first async slice (#16)?
> - Temporal is more mature for complex durable workflows but heavier.
> - A simple robust Postgres queue + worker might be sufficient and lower-risk initially.
>
> This choice should be framed as a decision to be made / prototyped in the deferred hardening plan #16, not presented as the default. The pull-based worker integration is clever, but the orchestration backend needs more explicit comparison of trade-offs (operational complexity, maturity, debugging experience for long jobs).

**Inline comment 3 — BYO operational complexity** (targeting the `byo_worker` description):

> **BYO operational / support burden for the primary beachhead (academies).**
>
> The pull-based `byo_worker` + SkyPilot design is excellent for security (no creds in orchestrator, outbound-only, user owns the compute).
>
> For the target users (Indian badminton academies/coaches per PMF research), however, requiring them to "run and maintain this small agent on your VM/GPU" is a significant ongoing operational load (updates, monitoring, networking/firewall issues, "it stopped after our IT change" tickets).
>
> Please add a short note on operational model and support implications. Consider whether a narrow "zero-infra BYO" proxy path (with strict isolation) should be offered as a middle tier for users who want the privacy/BYO benefits without running their own worker.
>
> This doesn't change the direction but is a real adoption risk that belongs in the doc.

**Top-level summary comment** (visible on the main PR conversation):

> **Summary review comment (as Grok, second-in-command collaborator)**
>
> I did a deep pass on the PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md proposal + the COMMERCIALIZATION.md updates.
>
> **Overall**: Excellent, grounded strategy doc. Strong reuse of the existing registry pattern, excellent privacy/liability thinking, correct scoping (docs-only, defers implementation), and good competitive differentiation via true BYO storage/compute. Consistent with the scaffolding work we've been shipping.
>
> **Key asks in the inline review comments** (posted on the doc):
> 1. Make **v1 vs longer vision explicit** — basic shot detection, rallies, highlight videos, history, and coach-correction/golden loop = clear v1. Gait/biomechanics = strictly P4+ longer vision. Prevents scope blur.
> 2. **Challenge the Hatchet recommendation** — valid requirements, but the specific choice feels early. Frame as a decision for the #16 async slice with comparison to lighter alternatives (Arq, simple queue, Temporal).
> 3. Call out **operational/support burden** of the byo_worker model for academy users. Suggest considering a narrow zero-infra proxy middle path.
>
> Other minor points (BYO threat model, etc.) are in the line comments.
>
> Please address the main three and ping me — happy to take another look at the updates before we treat this as direction for the post-scaffolding phase (and before unblocking #16).
>
> This is important context and shouldn't get dropped as we shift focus to quality iterations with the new labeled videos. Good work on the research pass (41 sources).

---

The review is now live on the PR (https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/58). The inline comments are attached to the specific paragraphs/sections in the Markdown diff, and the top-level comment records the overall context + explicit request for another review pass.

You (or anyone) can reply directly on GitHub, and the feedback is preserved in the thread.

Let me know when you've addressed things (or if you want me to adjust the wording of any comment before you respond on the PR). I'm ready to re-review the updates.

{"number":58,"title":"docs: multi-user \u0026#34;assistant coach\u0026#34; platform architecture strategy","body":"## What\n\n`docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md` — a research + strategy doc for evolving the tool from single-user/local into a multi-tenant **\u0026#34;assistant coach\u0026#34; platform** with **decoupled, pluggable layers** (storage and compute are user-provided). Strategy only — **no code**. Grounded in the current architecture, the monetization/PMF docs, and a 41-source external research pass.\n\n## Recommended architecture (headlines)\n\n- **Layered control-plane / data-plane split** — identity, ingestion, storage, orchestration, execution/compute, the (existing) pipeline, and per-user data-serving, each behind a small interface.\n- **Two new pluggable seams** mirroring the existing registry pattern:\n  - **`StorageBackend`** — build over **`fsspec`** (+ `smart_open` for large-MP4 streaming) for local/Drive/S3/GCS/Azure; **BYO-storage** via **STS `AssumeRole` + presigned URLs** so raw video moves browser↔the user\u0026#39;s bucket directly and we never store their long-lived keys.\n  - **`ComputeBackend`** — local / hosted GPU / **BYO-VM via an outbound pull-based worker** (GitHub self-hosted-runner pattern) so the orchestrator never holds the user\u0026#39;s cloud creds; **SkyPilot** for on-demand GPU in the user\u0026#39;s own cloud.\n- **Coach-first multi-tenant data model** (org/academy → coach → athlete → user); **highlights become a first-class persisted entity** + per-user history. Recommend **Postgres + `tenant_id` + Row-Level Security** (hosted); SQLite-per-tenant for self-hosted single-academy.\n- **Orchestration: Hatchet** (Postgres-backed durable jobs, resumable long GPU jobs) — the multi-user superset of `DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md` #16.\n- **Privacy:** the load-bearing nuance — **shot/highlight detection = general analytics (not Art. 9); gait that builds a per-athlete identifying template = Art. 9 biometric** needing explicit consent; minors/parental consent for academies; **BYO-storage as the liability lever**; **Gemini via Vertex AI** (no-train), not the consumer tier.\n- **Differentiator (validated):** none of Veo/Hudl/SwingVision/Spiideo offer true BYO-storage/compute (SwingVision does *edge* phone compute) — **\u0026#34;your video, your storage, your compute\u0026#34;** is genuinely novel for privacy-sensitive academies.\n\n## Phased roadmap\nP0 async + Postgres/RLS tenancy → P1 multi-user + upload + highlight persistence + history → P2 `StorageBackend` + BYO-storage → P3 `ComputeBackend` + BYO-compute → P4 assistant-coach (shot detection, then gait behind consent). The doc ends with the **key decisions left to you** and risks.\n\n## Notes\n- Docs-only; wired into `docs/README.md` and `COMMERCIALIZATION.md` C12. 41 cited sources (fact-vs-synthesis tagged; validate before budget).\n- Branched off `master`, **no stacking** (one PR, one work item).\n- Does not start implementation; the first infra slice (async, #16) is still on hold pending your review of #53.\n\nhttps://claude.ai/code/session_01DKXGwTeJi6BDdvZgEV5JSA\n\n---\n_Generated by [Claude Code](https://claude.ai/code/session_01DKXGwTeJi6BDdvZgEV5JSA)_","state":"open","draft":false,"merged":false,"mergeable_state":"clean","html_url":"https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/58","user":{"login":"avidullu","id":1674877,"profile_url":"https://github.com/avidullu","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/1674877?v=4"},"head":{"ref":"claude/platform-architecture-research","sha":"757842c62387c29f74386d0d94f74e6939d8b8ef","repo":{"full_name":"avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer"}},"base":{"ref":"master","sha":"5f7e97a7d0f92c26b551c985f5639410f1f5199c","repo":{"full_name":"avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer"}},"additions":592,"deletions":16,"changed_files":3,"commits":9,"comments":2,"created_at":"2026-06-07T19:35:01Z","updated_at":"2026-06-08T05:49:28Z"}

diff --git a/docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md b/docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..dc83041
--- /dev/null
+++ b/docs/PLATFORM_ARCHITECTURE.md
@@ -0,0 +1,570 @@
+# Platform Architecture — Multi-User "Assistant Coach"
+
+**Status:** 📐 Strategy / research — for review. No code yet.
+**Date:** 2026-06-07 · **Owner:** avidullu
+
+**Purpose.** Map the path from today's **single-user, local** tool to a **multi-tenant
+"assistant coach" platform**: users upload videos, get highlights **stored and
+browsable by history**, and choose **where their data lives** (Google Drive / cloud
+bucket / local) and **where the compute runs** (our GPU / their VM). The organizing
+principle is to **decouple the layers** — identity, ingestion, storage, orchestration,
+execution/compute, the detection+highlight pipeline, and data-serving — behind small
+**pluggable interfaces**, reusing the registry pattern the codebase already uses for
+segmenters/providers/validators/sports/annotations.
+
+**Product framing (the north star *and* the v1 line).** This is an **assistant coach**,
+not just a clipper — but the strategy is **phased and unambiguous about scope**:
+- **v1 (concrete, near-term):** **rally detection → highlight-reel compilation → per-user
+  history → a coach/user correction loop that feeds the golden set** ("assistant-coach
+  *lite*"). Essentially today's pipeline made multi-user — this is the real v1 deliverable.
+- **Mid-term:** **rich rally/shot semantic metadata** (shot type, rally outcome) and the
+  **owned video-QA model** (§5A; §6 P4–P5).
+- **North-star vision (strictly future — *not* v1):** **gait / biomechanics, per-athlete
+  identifying templates, and deep coaching feedback under a supervising coach** (§6 P6).
+  Inspiring, but explicitly deferred — and the part that raises the **biometric** privacy
+  bar (§7).
+
+The buyer is the **academy/coach** (beachhead in
+[`PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md`](PMF_MARKET_RESEARCH.md)); **power users self-serve**. Two
+consequences ripple through the design: (1) the data model keeps an **optional coach →
+athlete overlay over a generic core** (§4.1) — not baked in; (2) gait/biometrics is
+**special-category data**, gated and deferred (§7).
+
+> **Competitive note (validated by research):** none of Veo / Hudl / SwingVision /
+> Spiideo offer true BYO-storage or BYO-compute — they're vendor-cloud SaaS
+> (SwingVision uniquely runs *edge* compute on the user's iPhone). So **"your video,
+> your storage, your compute"** is a genuine differentiator for privacy-sensitive
+> academies — and SwingVision proves user-side compute is commercially viable. All four
+> share the same **teams → athletes → clips** data model, which we adopt (§4).
+
+> Scope: this is a **direction-setting doc**. It does not start implementation; the
+> first infra slice (an async job model) is the on-hold #16 in
+> [`DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md`](DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md). Commercial constraints come
+> from [`COMMERCIALIZATION.md`](COMMERCIALIZATION.md) (C4 paid-LLM no-train; C12 SaaS
+> foundations) and `archives/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md`.
+
+## 1. Current state & gaps (what we're evolving from)
+
+| Concern | Today | Gap for multi-user |
+|---|---|---|
+| Identity / tenancy | none | no users, orgs, auth, or isolation |
+| Storage | local `filepath` string; output to `output_dir` | no abstraction; can't BYO Drive/bucket; raw video must be on the box |
+| Data model | `videos` keyed by file **MD5**, `play_segments`, `events`, `candidate_segments` (SQLite) | no owner/athlete link; **highlights aren't a first-class persisted entity** (compile writes a file and returns a path); no per-user history |
+| Execution | pipeline runs **in-process, synchronously** in `/api/process` | blocks; can't dispatch to a user's VM/GPU; no job status |
+| Pipeline | **already pluggable** (segmenter/provider/validator/sport/annotation registries) | reuse as-is — this layer is the model for the rest |
+| API | `/api/process`, `/api/query`, `/api/compile`, `/api/config`; no auth | needs auth, per-tenant scoping, async job + history endpoints |
+
+Grounding: `backend/infrastructure/database.py` (schema + `PRAGMA user_version`
+migration ladder, v1), `backend/main.py` (endpoints, sync flow), the registry pattern
+in `backend/pipeline/segmenters/base.py::SegmenterRegistry` and
+`backend/providers/base.py`.
+
+## 2. Target layered architecture
+
+Decouple into layers with a clean **control-plane vs data-plane** split — the
+orchestrator coordinates *metadata and jobs*; the heavy *bytes and GPU work* can live
+elsewhere (even in the user's own cloud).
+
+```
+                ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
+   CONTROL      │  Presentation / API  (auth, per-tenant)        │
+   PLANE        │  Identity & Tenancy  (org→coach→athlete→user)  │
+                │  Orchestration / Jobs (submit→status→result)   │
+                └───────────────┬───────────────┬────────────────┘
+                                │ dispatch       │ metadata
+                ┌───────────────▼───────┐  ┌─────▼───────────────┐
+   DATA         │  Execution / Compute  │  │  Data / Serving     │
+   PLANE        │  (local | BYO-VM |    │  │  (per-tenant DB:    │
+                │   hosted GPU)         │  │   videos, segments, │
+                │   runs the Pipeline:  │  │   highlights, jobs, │
+                │   detect → highlight  │  │   history)          │
+                └───────────┬───────────┘  └─────────────────────┘
+                            │ read input / write artifacts
+                ┌───────────▼───────────────────────────────────┐
+                │  Storage  (local upload | Google Drive | S3 |  │
+                │  GCS | Azure)  — raw video + highlight outputs │
+                └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
+```
+
+The two **new pluggable seams** are **Storage** and **Compute/Execution**; everything
+else either exists (pipeline) or is net-new infra (identity, orchestration, serving). A
+**model/training layer** — the owned generative video-QA model (§5A) — sits atop these,
+fed by Storage (training corpus), Data (labels), and Compute (training + inference), and
+is delivered through the existing **AI-provider registry with a paid fallback**.
+
+## 3. Pluggable abstractions (extend the existing registry pattern)
+
+The codebase already proves the model: an `ABC` + a `Registry` singleton with
+`register/get/list_available` (e.g. `SegmenterRegistry`). Add two more registries with
+the same shape so they're plug-and-play and testable in isolation.
+
+### 3.1 `StorageBackend` registry
+Replace the bare `video_path: str` that flows through the pipeline with a
+**`StorageRef`** (a backend id + a key/URI), resolved by a backend.
+
+```python
+class StorageBackend(ABC):
+    name: str
+    def open_read(self, ref: StorageRef) -> BinaryIO: ...      # stream large video
+    def download_to_local(self, ref: StorageRef, dst: str): ...  # for ffmpeg/cv2/WSL
+    def upload(self, local_path: str, ref: StorageRef): ...     # persist highlight/artifacts
+    def signed_url(self, ref: StorageRef, ttl: int) -> str: ... # hand to LLM / browser
+    def stat(self, ref: StorageRef) -> StorageStat: ...
+    def list(self, prefix: StorageRef) -> list[StorageRef]: ...
+```
+
+- **Recommended implementation:** build the backend over **`fsspec`** (one matrix —
+  local / S3 (`s3fs`) / GCS (`gcsfs`) / Azure (`adlfs`) + Google Drive via `gdrivefs`,
+  Dropbox via `dropboxdrivefs`), reaching for **`smart_open`** as the robust large-MP4
+  byte-streaming primitive. Storage becomes one more registry. (Treat Drive/Dropbox as
+  add-ons — less battle-tested than `s3fs`/`gcsfs`; cover with integration tests.)
+- **Backends:** `local` (upload dir), `gdrive`, `s3`, `gcs`, `azure`. **BYO-storage**:
+  the tenant registers their own bucket/Drive; raw video **never leaves their account**.
+- **Auth — never persist users' long-lived keys.** For buckets, have the user create an
+  IAM role you **`AssumeRole`** into, scoped to one bucket/prefix, and mint **presigned
+  URLs** so raw video moves **browser ↔ the user's bucket directly, never through our
+  servers**. For Drive, OAuth with the narrow **`drive.file`** scope; for academies, a
+  **service account with domain-wide delegation**. Store any per-tenant creds encrypted.
+- **Why it matters for the pipeline:** ffmpeg/OpenCV/WSL need a **local file**, while the
+  LLM provider can take a **signed URL**. The backend exposes both `download_to_local`
+  (for compute) and `signed_url` (for the provider/browser), so the pipeline stops caring
+  *where* the bytes live.
+- **Migration path:** introduce `StorageRef` alongside the current local path; a `local`
+  backend wraps today's behavior so nothing breaks; adapters land incrementally.
+
+### 3.2 `ComputeBackend` / `ExecutionBackend` registry
+Where the pipeline actually runs for a given job.
+
+- **`local`** — in-process / local subprocess (today's behavior; dev + self-host).
+- **`hosted`** — our GPU VM pool (the SaaS default).
+- **`byo_worker`** — the tenant runs a **pull-based worker** (a small agent) on their own
+  VM/GPU. **Critical security property:** the worker *pulls* jobs scoped to its tenant
+  from the control plane, pulls input from the tenant's **own** storage, runs the
+  pipeline, and writes artifacts + results back — so **the orchestrator never holds the
+  user's cloud credentials** and needs **no inbound access** to their network. This
+  mirrors the **self-hosted CI runner** pattern (GitHub Actions runners use a 50s
+  outbound-only HTTPS long-poll). This pull-based runner is the **cheapest, most
+  credential-safe v1** for BYO-compute.
+- For users who want the platform to **provision GPUs in their *own* cloud account on
+  demand** (an academy with an AWS account but no standing GPU box), add **SkyPilot**
+  (BYOC: everything launches in the user's accounts/VPCs; creds stay with them).
+  **Modal** is the managed fallback for users wanting zero infra (accepting that raw
+  video then flows through Modal).
+- **⚠️ Operational / support burden (a real adoption risk).** For the primary beachhead —
+  Indian badminton **academies/coaches** — asking them to *run and maintain a worker on
+  their own VM/GPU* is a meaningful ongoing load (updates, monitoring, firew

## 5. Orchestration / async jobs

This is the serving layer that makes multi-user real. It is the **multi-user superset of
[`DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md`](DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md) #16** (async job model) — build
that first, single-tenant, then generalize.

- **Job record** (`jobs` table): `id, tenant_id, video_id, type (index|compile|analyze),
  status (queued|running|done|failed), progress, compute_backend, result_ref, error,
  timestamps`. `/api/process` returns **202 + job_id**; `/api/jobs/{id}` polls.
- **Idempotency** keyed on `(tenant_id, video_id, pipeline_version)` — reuse the
  content-hash discipline already in `compute_video_id`.
- **Dispatch:** the orchestrator enqueues; a worker (hosted or `byo_worker`) claims jobs
  for its tenant, resolves input via the tenant's `StorageBackend`, runs the pipeline,
  writes artifacts + DB rows. **Observability reports** (already per-video) become the
  job artifact surfaced in history.
- **Queue/worker backend — a decision to prototype in #16, *not* a default here.** The
  *requirements* are firm — **idempotency**, **resumability** (a 40-min GPU job mustn't
  restart on a worker blip), **progress states** — which rule out fire-and-forget Celery/RQ
  for the long jobs. The *choice* should be made by prototyping in the
  [`DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md`](DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN.md) #16 slice, weighing:
  - **Start simplest:** **Arq** (async-native, natural FastAPI fit) or even a **plain
    Postgres-backed queue + worker** — likely sufficient for the *first* single-tenant
    async slice, with the lowest operational complexity and the easiest debugging.
  - **Durable-execution upgrade if/when needed:** **Hatchet** (Postgres-backed, per-step
    retries/timeouts/concurrency; pairs neatly with the pull-based worker) — attractive
    because it adds **no new datastore** once we're on Postgres, but it *is* a new
    dependency + operational/debugging surface; adopt only when long-job resumability/
    observability demands it.
  - **Heaviest:** **Temporal** — most mature durable workflows, operationally heavy;
    reserve for genuinely complex multi-stage pipelines at scale.

  Record the decision criteria in #16: operational complexity, maturity, **debugging
  experience for long GPU jobs**, and whether durability is worth a new dependency.
  **Bias: start with the simplest thing that gives retries + status; graduate only on
  evidence.**

## 5A. Owned generative model (the video-QA assistant) & the data flywheel

## 5A. Owned generative model (the video-QA assistant) & the data flywheel

The north-star capability: an **owned, self-hostable generative video model** —
fine-tuned on sports video (including **consented, user-contributed** footage) — that
**answers questions about rallies, shots, and technique**. This is the conversational
brain of the "assistant coach." Crucially, it does **not** replace the known stack; it
is introduced **behind the same abstraction, with the paid API as an always-ready
fallback**.

> **The moat is *not* the model.** Anyone can download Qwen-VL/Gemma. The defensible
> assets are **(a) the consented, badminton-specific video→QA dataset** and **(b) the
> eval harness**. Build and message *those* — not "we own an LLM."

> **✅ Committed guardrail (legal, non-negotiable).** We respect the licensing findings in
> full. **Paid frontier models (Gemini / OpenAI / Anthropic) are used for *inference /
> serving / fallback* only — never as a *training* data source.** Harvesting their outputs
> to train our owned model would breach their "no competing model" terms (a terms/copyright
> violation), so it is prohibited in both engineering and legal process. **Training teachers
> are open, Apache-2.0 models only** (Qwen-VL / InternVL / Gemma 4). Detail in the
> distillation constraint below.

### Core principle: AI is a pluggable provider with a paid fallback
The codebase already abstracts the cloud AI behind `provider_registry` /
`AIVideoProvider` (`backend/providers/`). Make that a **first-class platform principle**:
- Every generative capability routes through the **provider registry**.
- The **owned model** plugs in as a new provider (`owned_vlm`); **Gemini (paid, via
  Vertex AI)** stays registered as the **fallback** (failover on error/low-confidence,
  or as the quality ceiling/teacher reference).
- This lets us **ship today on the known paid stack** and **swap in the owned model when
  its quality clears a measured bar** — with zero architectural churn and the paid path
